Proposition 4

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {H}$$\end{document}H be a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi _0\in \mathbb {S}(\mathscr {H})$$\end{document}Ψ0∈S(H), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi _t=\exp (-i\hat{H}t)\Psi _0$$\end{document}Ψt=exp(-iH^t)Ψ0 with self-adjoint \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{H}$$\end{document}H^, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g:\mathbb {S}(\mathscr {H})\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}g:S(H)→R be any bounded measurable function. Then the time average \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{g(\Psi _t)}$$\end{document}g(Ψt)¯ exists.

The proof can be found in Appendix G.

Application to Statement 5

Derivation of Statement 5. We assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{H}$$\end{document}H^ satisfies the ETH (126)–(127), and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_G$$\end{document}DG is not too large. Then Proposition 2 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {H}_R = \mathscr {H}_{\textrm{gmc}}$$\end{document}HR=Hgmc yields that for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi _0\in \mathbb {S}(\mathscr {H}_\textrm{gmc})$$\end{document}Ψ0∈S(Hgmc), for most times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in [0,\infty )$$\end{document}t∈[0,∞), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S \approx {{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}^{\overline{S}} \hat{\rho }_\textrm{gmc}$$\end{document}ρ^ΨtS≈trS¯ρ^gmc. Statement 1a tells us that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{tr}\,}}^{\overline{S}}\hat{\rho }_\textrm{gmc}\approx \hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S$$\end{document}trS¯ρ^gmc≈ρ^gGS. We thus obtain the first statement (69) of Statement 5.

We now turn to the conditional wave function. Theorem 2 in [18] shows that for every t, most orthonormal bases b of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {H}^{\overline{S}}$$\end{document}HS¯ are such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }^S_{\Psi _t}}$$\end{document}LψS(t)≈GAPρ^tS. By the continuous dependence (102) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }}$$\end{document}GAPρ^ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\rho }$$\end{document}ρ^, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }^S_{\Psi _t}}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S}$$\end{document}GAPρ^tS≈GAPρ^gGS for most t and regardless of the basis b. Thus,132\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \forall \Psi _0\ {\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t\ge 0\ {\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b:\ \mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∀Ψ0⋁|t≥0⋁|b:LψS(t)≈GAPρ^gGS.We want to move the “\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b” to the left of “\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t$$\end{document}⋁|t” because we want to apply the same basis b at all times t. As discussed before Proposition 3, even commuting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t$$\end{document}⋁|t and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b requires further assumptions, to which we turn now. We express that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}$$\end{document}LψS(t) is close to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }^S_{\textrm{gG}}}$$\end{document}GAPρ^gGS by means of integrating them against arbitrary “test” functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f:\mathbb {S}(\mathscr {H}^S)\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}f:S(HS)→R as in (104). Proposition 3 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=ONB(\mathscr {H}^{\overline{S}})$$\end{document}X=ONB(HS¯), x=bx=b, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(b,t)=|\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}(f) - \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }^S_{\textrm{gG}}}(f)|$$\end{document}F(b,t)=|LψS(t)(f)-GAPρ^gGS(f)| (which satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le F(b,t) \le C=2\Vert f\Vert _\infty $$\end{document}0≤F(b,t)≤C=2‖f‖∞) allows us to interchange \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t$$\end{document}⋁|t and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b, as its hypothesis (130) is satisfied by virtue of Proposition 4 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g(\Psi )=|\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S}(f)-\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }^S_{\textrm{gG}}}(f)|$$\end{document}g(Ψ)=|LψS(f)-GAPρ^gGS(f)| (note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi ^S$$\end{document}ψS depends on Ψ Ψ) for arbitrary but fixed b and f; after all, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(b,t)=g(\Psi _t)$$\end{document}F(b,t)=g(Ψt). Thus,133\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \forall \Psi _0\ {\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b \ {\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t\ge 0 : \ \mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S}\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∀Ψ0⋁|b⋁|t≥0:LψS(t)≈GAPρ^gGS,which is what we claimed in the case of typical ONBs b; the case of ONBs diagonalizing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{Q}_1,\ldots ,\hat{Q}_{K-1}$$\end{document}Q^1,…,Q^K-1 is analogous, while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b now means “for most ONBs b diagonalizing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{Q}_1,\ldots ,\hat{Q}_{K-1}$$\end{document}Q^1,…,Q^K-1” instead of “for most ONBs b.” This completes the derivation of Statement 5.

An alternative strategy of the derivation starts from the fact, mentioned above, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\forall \Psi _0\, \forall t\ge 0\, {\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b: \, \mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S}$$\end{document}∀Ψ0∀t≥0⋁|b:LψS(t)≈GAPρ^ΨtS. Now we try first, before replacing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S$$\end{document}ρ^ΨtS by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\rho }_{\textrm{gG}}^S$$\end{document}ρ^gGS, to move “\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b” to the left of the t-quantifier. It must still be expected that “∀ t∀t” has to be replaced by the weaker “\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t$$\end{document}⋁|t” because it may well happen that every b has some bad, exceptional t’s [17]. We apply Proposition 4 to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g(\Psi )=|\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S}(f)-\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_{\Psi }^S}(f)|$$\end{document}g(Ψ)=|LψS(f)-GAPρ^ΨS(f)|, then Proposition 3 to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=ONB(\mathscr {H}^{\overline{S}})$$\end{document}X=ONB(HS¯), x=bx=b, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F(b,t)=|\mathscr {L}_{\psi ^S(t)}(f)-\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S}(f)|=g(\Psi _t)$$\end{document}F(b,t)=|LψS(t)(f)-GAPρ^ΨtS(f)|=g(Ψt), so we can interchange \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t$$\end{document}⋁|t and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}b$$\end{document}⋁|b. Now if each of two statements \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_1(t)$$\end{document}s1(t) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_2(t)$$\end{document}s2(t) is true for (1-ɛ )(1-ε)-most t, then “\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_1(t)$$\end{document}s1(t) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_2(t)$$\end{document}s2(t)” is true for at least (1-2ɛ )(1-2ε)-most t. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textstyle \bigvee \hspace{-2.6mm} | \,\,}t: \hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S \approx \hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S$$\end{document}⋁|t:ρ^ΨtS≈ρ^gGS by (69) and for those t, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_{\Psi _t}^S}\approx \textrm{GAP}_{\hat{\rho }_\textrm{gG}^S}$$\end{document}GAPρ^ΨtS≈GAPρ^gGS by (102), we obtain (133), as claimed.
